# P R E F A C E.

## To the Lovers of the Light and Children of the Truth.

The most noble and highly precious mouth of our most dearly beloved **LORD JESUS** Christ has, with his own present voice, spoken nothing but unspeakably gracious and—beyond all heart's desire—kind and friendly words to poor humanity. These same most lovingly intended words, which issued from his living mouth while he in person—or rather the complete fullness of the essential <term>Godhead|The Greek term 'theotēs', referring to the divine nature or essence of God.</term> <note>original: "wesentlichen Gottheit"</note> in bodily form—walked upon this earth in his most holy visible form, were most diligently written down for our comfort by the <term>Evangelists|The traditional authors of the four Gospels: Matthew, Mark, Luke, and John.</term>, especially John. Rather, this holy, holy, holy Light, which illuminates all people who come into this world <note>A reference to the Gospel of John 1:9: "The true light that gives light to everyone was coming into the world."</note>, has also from one time to another, among all nations, equipped various people—adorned with joyful, blissful gifts of light—as witness-messengers of His overflowing goodness, and presented them publicly to the world for its service. With this highly prized gift, He has also not left us High and Low Germans <note>Referring to the speakers of both High German (the south) and Low German (the north) dialects.</note> unbestowed. 

For to say nothing now of other light-filled German men, such as *Albertus Magnus* <term>Albertus Magnus|A 13th-century German Dominican friar and polymath, later canonized, who was widely believed in the Renaissance to have mastered alchemy.</term>, *Tauler* <term>Johannes Tauler|A famous 14th-century German mystic whose works emphasized the inner life and the "ground of the soul."</term>, *Paracelsus* <term>Paracelsus|A revolutionary 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist who sought to combine medicine with chemistry.</term>, *Basil Valentine* <term>Basil Valentine|The name attributed to a legendary 15th-century alchemist, now thought to be a pseudonym for 16th-century authors; he is famous for his work on antimony.</term>, etc., and only to remember at present the excellent philosopher **JOHANN ISAAC HOLLANDUS**.

### Preface.

...sets down, and <term>collated<note>original: *collationiret*; the scholarly process of comparing different versions of a manuscript to identify errors and variations.</note></term> it with another handwritten copy, and <term>emended<note>original: *emendiret*; to correct or improve a text by removing errors.</note></term> it as much as possible; which I have herewith released for printing for the benefit of the enthusiasts. I do also remember that the contents of this **Little Vegetable Work** <note>original: *Vegetabel-Wercklein*; "vegetable" in alchemy refers to the kingdom of plants and their specific medicinal or transmutative properties.</note> are partially to be found printed in the German book called **True Alchemy** <note>original: *Alchimia vera*</note> or **The Mountain of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Mons Philosophorum*</note>; however, I perceived there much that was mutilated and not a little that was entirely omitted, and for that reason, I have accelerated the printing of this version all the more. ### Preface. 9

on / since it can be perceived from manifest **Divine** Providence that **GOD** through His gracious guidance has led us together; for He alone knows the <term>souls<note>original: *Gemüther*; refers to the inner character, disposition, or spiritual state of a person.</note>,</term> while for us humans such a thing is impossible. It was my intention to share with them the <term>mine and the fountain<note>original: *fodin und fontin*; likely derived from the Latin *fodina* (mine) and *fontina* (little fountain). Similarly, other praiseworthy persons have offered themselves and their spirits, eager for the <term>Art<note>original: *Kunst-begieriges Gemüth*; specifically referring to the "Hermetic Art" or alchemy.</note></term>, to me. However, a doubt always arose soon thereafter, either in them or in me, and in this way, the matter was hindered. I will therefore henceforth abandon such <term>meddling curiosity<note>original: *Fürwitzes*; implies a sort of over-eager or impertinent striving to know or do something before its proper time.</note></term> and leave everything to **Divine** Governance. I wish to let this be known as a friendly notice to those who have searched for me until now. To them, I must also necessarily make this known: that I understand some have recently appeared who have boasted of having attained my acquaintance and intimacy—

# Preface.

I can give no other testimony to such things than that it is all the pure, plain truth; and not only by <term>Hollandus<note>Referring to Isaac Hollandus or John Isaac Hollandus, legendary 15th-century alchemists.</note></term>, but also by other philosophers who <insert>truly possessed</insert> the <term>Philosophical Quintessence<note>original: *Philosophische Quintam Essentiam*; the "Fifth Essence," a substance believed by alchemists to be the purest form of matter, capable of curing all diseases and perfecting metals.</note></term> and <insert>have experienced this many times and found it to be true</insert>, as I will provide in more detail at the end of this little treatise. And indeed, all of this is very easy to believe for anyone who merely reflects upon and takes to heart the transcendent, inexhaustible love of God toward His own image, mankind. Indeed, he finds that such wonders are as nothing compared to the abyss of love provided for the earnest believer in Christ even before the foundation for the <term>creation<note>original: *Schöpfung*</note></term> of this entire world was called forth and laid down. Concerning this, I must also fall silent, because no eye has seen, nor ear heard, nor has it entered into any human heart, what God has prepared for those who faithfully love Him. <note>A reference to the biblical passage 1 Corinthians 2:9.</note> Therefore, there is no possibility of wishing for anything better from God than this: that one should love Him in the **LORD JESUS CHRIST** with all one’s heart, with all one’s soul, with all one’s mind, and with all one’s strength, and also love all other men as

# Preface.

what could I, a worthless little worm <note>original: *Würmlein*; a common 17th-century Pietist expression used to emphasize human frailty and humility in the presence of the Divine.</note>, then imagine for myself, I who am not even a tiny mote of sun-dust before so great a world? And yet You, the living fountain-spring of all love and kindness, have shown such great mercy and grace toward me, such a lowly creature, even from my mother’s womb, that I can never count it all, much less thank You enough for it. 

But I thank You for such great, innumerable compassion from my whole heart in the deepest humility; especially, however, that You have shown me the grace that I—according to my weakness—heartily love You. And such love, flowing from You, drives me so that I would also gladly do what is dear to You, namely Your noble, alone-good will and pleasure.

# MASTER JOHN ISAAC HOLLANDUS.

## The Vegetable Work.

### CHAPTER I.

<image-desc>Decorative ornamental woodcut initial 'A' featuring vine and leaf motifs in a square frame.</image-desc>Now I will teach and describe the secrets of the arts, which secret precedes all secrets hidden in the art of <term>Alchemy</term> <note>original: *Alchimy*; the ancient branch of natural philosophy focused on the transmutation of matter, particularly the purification of substances.</note>; for here one will understand the wondrous works which **GOD** has wrought in all things He made from the four elements. For I will teach you here to recognize the spirits of all herbs, trees, and all growing things, and to separate them from their bodies, as well as to purify the four <term>Elements</term> <note>original: *Elementa*; refers to the classical elements of Earth, Air, Fire, and Water, which alchemists believed could be separated and recombined.</note>, and to return them to their first essence and to their perfect power—that is, once the elements are purified, how one should put them back together again and make from them a perfect and fixed body; then it is glorified and performs wondrous effects, of which enough for now, etc.

### CHAPTER II.

Now I will teach you to extract and make <term>Armenian Salt</term> <note>original: *Sal Armeniacum*; traditionally known as Sal Ammoniac (ammonium chloride), a vital chemical in alchemy used for cleaning metals and in various chemical reactions.</note> from all growing things, the

->John Isaac Hollandus<-

from all things that have the four elements within them, you can extract <term>Armenian Salt</term> <note>original: *Salarmeniac*; traditionally known as Sal Ammoniac (ammonium chloride). In this context, Hollandus views it as a universal spirit found in all matter.</note>, because the spirit of all things is Armenian salt; therefore, Armenian salt is designated and represented like the Sun: For the Sun is the highest sign and the most powerful planet of the heavens, since the Sun illuminates every thing in its essence, and it warms the whole world, and much more, which is impossible to describe here: Therefore <term>Salmiac</term> <note>A common contraction of *Sal Armeniacum*.</note> is compared to the Sun, and it is a wondrous thing, for without it one can bring nothing to <term>perfection</term> <note>original: *perfection*; the state of reaching a completed, purified, or "gold-like" alchemical result.</note> in Alchemy, just as without the Sun no thing or fruit can become ripe; so too, without the Salmiac, no work in Alchemy can be brought to completeness; For Armenian salt is able to unite all things that are contrary to one another and refuse to mix, so that they afterwards mix and <term>conjoin</term> <note>original: *conjungiren*; to chemically or spiritually unite disparate substances into a single body.</note>: This is thus one of its powers, according to this example: The Sun has such power in the mountains and in the <term>minerals</term> <note>original: *Mineren*; refers to ores and substances found within the earth.</note>, that through its heat and power, which God has bestowed upon it, it can equalize and unite all unequal and contrary things, however great they may be. For what is more contrary than heat and cold, dryness and moisture, water and fire? Nevertheless, the Sun is able through its heat and power to equalize all these things, and to unite them in such a way that they shall now and nevermore separate from one another; that is <term>Sulphur</term> <note>In alchemy, Sulphur represents the soul, the oily principle, and the quality of combustibility.</note> and <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurius*; represents the spirit, the watery principle, and the quality of volatility.</note>, which

### Vegetable Work

**Sulfur** is hot and dry, while **Mercury** is cold and moist; but the Sun, through its heat and power, unites the <term>🜍</term> <note>symbol for sulfur</note> sulfur with the **Mercury**, from which **gold**, **silver**, **lead**, and **copper** are then generated. Depending on how the <term>ores</term> <note>original: *Mineren*; the raw mineral deposits in the earth.</note> lie and whether the earth is good or bad, the metals are generated accordingly. However, it is the Sun that must "cook" everything; and it cooks the metals better in one place than in another because it shines hotter in one location than in another, since one landscape is more <term>temperate</term> <note>original: *temperirter*; referring to the alchemical balance of qualities (hot, cold, wet, dry).</note> than another, which is determined by the **planets** under which a land is situated. In a place where the Sun shines so hotly, it cannot cook moderately enough on account of the great heat, but it is nevertheless cooked in the end. Conversely, where the Sun shines too coldly, the cooking there is <term>slow and tedious</term> <note>original: *langweilig*; in this period, it means a long duration or a drawn-out process rather than "boring."</note>; but where the Sun shines temperately—that is, neither too hot nor too cold—and is under a good **planet** (meaning a well-tempered land with good earth), there it is cooked moderately and generated into **gold** or **silver**. In such a manner it also behaves with **Salmiac** <note>A shortened form of Sal Ammoniac.</note>: it must unite, bind, mix, and equalize all contrary things through its temperate heat. For where the **Salarmeniac** is too hot, it will cook all the longer; if it is too cold, it will also cook that much longer; but if it is well

->and achieve a higher **projection**<-

# Johannes Isaac Hollandus

### CHAPTER III.

How should one now understand this—that one *Sal Armoniacum* <note>original Latin: *Sal Armoniacum*; Sal Ammoniac or Ammonium Chloride, a volatile salt central to alchemical practice.</note> is too hot and the other too cold? You should understand it like this: herbs are unequal; one is cold, the other hot, and yet they both contain *Salarmeniac* within them. But one *Salarmeniac* is better than the other; nevertheless, both have the power to "cook" <note>In alchemy, "cooking" or *decoction* refers to the prolonged application of heat to mature, ripen, or perfect a substance.</note> in this Art, to perform a *projection* <term>projection</term> <note>The final stage of the Great Work, where the alchemical catalyst is "cast" upon a base metal to transmute it into gold or silver.</note>, and to create a "binding"—that is, to unite and mix two contrary things. 

It also happens at times that the *Salarmeniac* is not well-cleansed or purified, and that the <term>untempered</term> <note>original: *untemperirte*; referring to an imbalance of the four primary qualities: hot, cold, wet, and dry.</note> heat which the herbs possess is not properly "killed" or purged. If one produces the *Salarmeniac* from such material, that "poisonous heat" is harmful to it, so that the power of the *projection* is diminished. It is exactly the same with the cold herbs: if some [impurity] remains within them, the *projection* will be just as much diminished and smaller. This is the difference or the distinction: why a *Salmiac* made from one species <note>original: *Specie*; a specific type of botanical or mineral substance.</note> or type of herb does not perform an equal *projection*. 

However, this is the fault of the **laborant** <note>A laboratory worker or alchemical practitioner.</note> who made the *Salmiac*, because he did not purify it well and did not **distill it over** <note>original: *übergezogen*; likely referring to sublimation, where the salt is heated until it rises as a vapor and solidifies at the top of the vessel, leaving impurities behind.</note> often enough. For whoever wishes to make the *Salarmeniac* correctly, as it ought to be, must distill it over and again, and yet again, until nothing remains behind and it becomes as white as snow. Then the *Salarmeniac* is rightly tem-

<summary>The author explains that the quality of Sal Ammoniac depends on the herbs it is extracted from and, more importantly, the skill of the alchemist in purifying it. Repeated sublimation is required to reach the "tempered" state necessary for successful transmutation.</summary>

<keywords>Sal Ammoniac, Salmiac, projection, purification, sublimation, tempering, herbal salts, alchemy, transmutation</keywords>

Likewise, the evil, poisonous cold that was in the herb shall then offer no further hindrance, for everything is now purified and tempered <term>tempered</term> <note>original: *temperirt*; the alchemical state where the four primary qualities (hot, cold, wet, dry) are brought into perfect, non-conflicting balance.</note>, having left behind all that was untempered.

### CHAPTER IV.

Furthermore, my child should know that all things which God has created from the four elements must die, pass away, and be purified; but afterward, they must be created anew and born once again. Then, these re-created and newborn things shall never die again, nor pass away, nor shall anyone be able to spoil them or bring them to ruin. No fire shall be able to burn them; rather, they will henceforth endure for eternity because they have returned to their first power <note>original: *Krafft*; the inherent vital energy or divine essence endowed by the Creator.</note>, which God gave them when He created and made all things in the beginning.

And following the sin committed by our first parents, Adam and Eve <note>original Latin: *Hevæ*.</note>, God the Lord was so angered that He made the four elements corrupt and perishable, so that they must now all die and come to nothing. Just as God the Lord Himself spoke to Adam when He gave him this commandment, saying: Adam, you may eat of all things that are in Paradise, except for this tree, for it is the Tree of Life <note>original: *Holz des Lebens*; literally "Wood of Life."</note>. And I—

<summary>The author explains that alchemical purification is a process of "rebirth" that restores matter to its incorruptible state before the Fall of Man. This theological framework justifies the alchemist's work as a restoration of the "first power" given by God.</summary>

<keywords>Sal Ammoniac, purification, four elements, spiritual alchemy, Fall of Man, Adam and Eve, Tree of Life, incorruptibility, tempering</keywords>

Johannis Isaci Hollandi

<meta>catchword: the</meta>

[say]ing to you, Adam, thus: "If you eat from this tree, you and all that is created from the four elements shall die the death; I swear this." God swore an oath there, of which we have all become well aware; therefore, everything created and made from the four elements must now die. This includes all humans, all animals, and everything that has received a physical body or possesses sensitivity <note>original: *empfindlichkeit*; refers to the ability to perceive or feel, a trait used to distinguish "sentient" life from "insentient" life like stones.</note> within it—indeed, even those things which have no sensation in themselves, such as herbs and trees, and everything made from the four elements. For even if some have no feeling, they nevertheless live and bring forth their fruits just like the sentient creatures. The herbs bring forth fruit and their seeds, or whatever natures God has implanted in them; the trees bear apples and pears, or whatever the Lord God has assigned to them. 

Thus live all herbs, all trees, and all other things that the Lord God created from the four elements. And the Lord God has given a spirit to all trees, all herbs, and all other things He created from the four elements. And as many different things or herbs as there may be from the four elements, the Lord God has given to each one of them its own particular spirit of unique power <term>power</term> <note>original: *Krafft*; in an alchemical context, this refers to the "virtue" or medicinal potency of a substance.</note> and of unique nature. Each also has its own particular body and form, and that same body is likewise composed of the four elements; and one herb is cold, the other hot, the third moist, the fourth dry, according—

It establishes that every created thing possesses a unique "spirit" and "power" regardless of whether it is sentient or insentient.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Chapter V, expanding on the concept introduced at the end of the previous page: the "aging of the world" (Mundus Senescens). The author explains why modern medicine and alchemy are more difficult than in the days of the Biblical patriarchs, attributing the decline in human lifespan to the weakening of the "spirits" within plants due to elemental decay.</meta>

<header>Johann Isaac Hollandus</header>

When God the Lord first cast and drove Adam out of Paradise, the herbs had greater power than they have today. This is because the world is growing old, and for that reason humans no longer live as long as in former times; and this is on account of the current old age of the world, and because the sun, together with the planets, lose their powers through the world's age and the contamination of the elements. Therefore, the spirits within the herbs are now so suppressed by the infection <note>In a pre-germ theory sense, "infection" here means the corruption or staining of the pure elemental qualities.</note> of the elements that they can no longer manifest their powers in humans as they surely did in former times. 

If the spirits could still reveal their powers now as they did in olden times, humans would still live to 200 years and more today, and would possess such strength in everything as they formerly had. However, humans are now also weak and delicate, and they would not be able to bear the spirits of the herbs if those were still of their former potency; rather, they would have to take and use them in a tempered <term>tempered</term> <note>original: *temperirt*; meaning diluted, balanced, or moderated so as not to overwhelm the weakened human constitution.</note> manner. If they did that, they would live far longer still and remain rejuvenated. 

For this reason, one must kill and annihilate all herbs and other things, and bring them to powder and to ash, and finally to water <note>This describes the alchemical stages of calcination (reduction to ash) and dissolution (reduction to "water" or liquid state).</note>; afterwards, join the soul or the spirit back into it, and make from it a perfect body. Thus you have an earthly treasure that is better than gold and silver or precious stones; for you have a <term>perfectly glorified body</term> <note>original: *perfect glorificirtes corpus*; a substance that has been purified of its "elemental infection" and recombined into an incorruptible state.</note>, which shall never perish, but eternally

<summary>The author explains that the decay of the world since the Fall of Man has weakened the medicinal power of herbs and shortened human lifespans. He argues that alchemy can overcome this by "killing" the raw matter and reuniting its spirit with a purified body to create a perfect, incorruptible medicine.</summary>

## CHAPTER VI.

Now furthermore, my child, you should know / that I said in the preceding chapter / that one should kill the herbs and let them die / and make from them a powder or ashes / this is to be understood as follows; One should draw off the evil, impure wateriness / or let it dry out by itself / which is best / or otherwise draw it off in a <term>water bath</term> <note>original: *Balneo Mariæ*; a gentle heating method using a vessel placed in boiling water, known today as a bain-marie.</note>, for the evil moisture is the main thing that hinders the spirit and keeps it subdued / so that it cannot manifest its power in humans or in metals, that is, in <term>mercury</term> <note>original: *☿*, the alchemical symbol for Mercury.</note> / and the wretched fools labor with the moisture / and even if it should happen by chance / that something good was done or found by them / the evil moisture takes away from them that which they sought / they set the herbs to <term>putrefy</term> <note>original: *putreficiren*; the alchemical process of controlled decay or fermentation intended to break down the physical form of a substance.</note>, draw them off afterward / and operate with the <term>putrefied material</term> so

This is the cause and the understanding / for which reason you must guard yourselves against the evil wateriness: A misfortune / which one knows of beforehand / is easier to prevent and avoid / and therefore I have told it to you.

### CHAPTER VII.

Furthermore, once you have now drawn off the evil moisture / and the herb is dry / then draw off the spirit <term>by descent</term> <note>original: *per descensum*; a method of distillation where the heat is applied from above and the vapor or oil is forced downward into a receiver.</note> / as I will teach you hereafter / and then <term>calcine</term> <note>to reduce a substance to a white powder or ash by prolonged heating.</note> the <term>body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*; the solid physical remains of the plant.</note> as white as snow / then you have two natures, namely body and spirit / then you may <term>dissolve</term> <note>original: *solviren*.</note> the spirit in <term>Water of Life</term> <note>original: *Aqua Vitæ*; a term for concentrated alcohol or brandy used as a solvent.</note>, which is very good / or in good distilled wine-vinegar /

### CHAPTER VIII:

### CHAPTER XV.

Now we shall return again to the separation of the four elements; regarding this, there are many different discourses and arguments, which would be too lengthy to recount here; I will conclude it with brief words. First, all herbs have within them the four elements; three of the elements are now visible and tangible—namely, water, earth, and fire—but the air is invisible. Water is difficult to separate from air, yet it is possible to do so through slow distillation in a <term>water bath</term> <note>Latin original: *Balneo Mariæ*; a vessel of water used to heat another container gently and evenly, preventing the substance from burning.</note>, with a small, mild, and gentle fire, so that nothing rises except the wateriness itself. However, if one applies a strong fire so that the water boils, the air would also ascend, whether in small or large amounts; but through a gentle fire, the water is to be separated from the air. Earth and air are also difficult to sunder from one another, but through a great, hot, strong, and long-lasting fire, one can indeed separate the earth from the air. If one applies a weak fire, the air would remain with the earth, which would then come to the element of fire; for the element of fire is the last to separate from the earth, and it must be separated from the earth through intense heat and long-lasting fire. For if any of the element of fire remained in the earth, then the air would not be separated from the earth, because fire and air are inseparable, although many fools who labor in this Art <note>The Art of Alchemy.</note> are of the

### CHAPTER XVI.

Thus I now call such learned people—of both 
spiritual and secular status—great fools, for 
they imagine themselves to have grasped the Art 
by means of certain books that treat the Art in a 
parabolic <note>original: "parabolischer weise"; referring to the use of allegories, metaphors, or riddles to hide alchemical secrets from the uninitiated.</note> manner. Thereupon they proceed

secular status, who have squandered all their 
possessions therein and become poor, so that they 
are rightly called fools. The unlearned, indeed, are 
not to be blamed for this, for they do not know what 
they are doing. And when they have become poor, 
the Art seems an impossibility to them; and according 
to their perception, this is also the truth, for the Art 
is impossible for <term>idiots</term> <note>original: "Idioten"; used here in the historical sense to mean uneducated laypeople or those lacking specialized knowledge.</note> and the unlearned to perform.

unlearned person? One could not even bring 
it within such a person’s understanding. Therefore, 
the Art seems impossible to an unlearned man,

indeed true: the Art is impossible for such

### CHAPTER XVII.

It is not required here to divide the elements from one 
another, each one particularly alone; 
rather, only to clean and to purify the elements. 
To that end, you shall separate the water from the air and 
from the earth, and from the fire; and then you 
shall separate the air and the fire from the 
earth, and make the air pure. The fire is pure in 
itself, but the fire must be operated with the 
air, by means of the air that is in the fire;

## CHAPTER XX.

Furthermore, if a person were to take a small amount of the *fifth essence* <note>original: *quinta Essentia*; in alchemy, this is the purest, most spiritualized form of a substance, believed to be the "spirit" that gives life.</note> every day in their food and drink, or in wine in the morning, that person would not die—unless nature itself were to expire—and they would remain in the same essence and condition as they were when they began to take the *fifth essence*. They would not appear older in the face, nor would their limbs become less agile, stiffer, or more bent, because the *fifth essence* would immediately drive out the evil <note>Here, "evil" refers to physical impurities or "dross" that cause aging and disease.</note> that a person might have within them. For wherever the *fifth essence* goes, no ailment or evil can remain; for this reason, it is called the *fifth essence* or the *Elixir* <note>original: *Elixir*; a legendary medicinal substance capable of curing all diseases and prolonging life.</note>.

And as soon as it has consumed and driven out the sickness, it makes the blood youthful again. Once the blood is rejuvenated, all the limbs become alert, quick, and strong again, and they remain always in one state. Such a person also has no need to fear any poison, for no poison can harm such a person.

I have seen for myself that an ounce of *arsenic* <note>original: *Arsenici*; a highly toxic mineral used in alchemical experiments.</note> was put into a glass with wine vinegar and given to a man to drink who had forfeited his life <note>A common alchemical anecdote involving a prisoner condemned to death being used as a test subject.</note>. Afterward, he was handed a glass of wine in which there was the weight of a grain of wheat of the *fifth essence* <note>original: *quintæ Essentiæ*</note>, and the poison did the man no harm. I asked him myself how it had affected him, and he answered me that he knew nothing of it and had felt nothing, even though already within a mo- <note>The text cuts off mid-word, likely meaning "moment" or "month."</note>

To those, I gave the *fifth essence* and brought them back to health within twenty-four hours. ## CHAPTER XXII.

Once the <term>corpse</term> <note>original: "Leichnam"; the solid, physical residue of the plant matter after initial processing.</note> has been separated from the <term>spirit</term> <note>original: "Geist"; the volatile or essence-carrying part of the substance.</note>, or the elements from the earth, or however you wish to understand it, you should cleanly skim off the oil floating on top with a feather. This oil must not be reunited with the earth or <term>body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note> again. Nothing else should be returned to the earth except the spirit that was drawn from it, or the <term>sal ammoniac</term> <note>original: "Salmiac"; in this context, it refers to the volatile salt or "fatty spirit" of the plant.</note>, as you best understand it; this is the fatty spirit I mean, which THE LORD GOD will give back to the bodies and pour into them. 

Thus you should learn to understand when I say that you should take one thing away from it that is in excess, and restore to it what it lacks. By this I mean that you should take the evil away from it—that is, the evil heat or poisonous cold—by <term>distilling</term>, or by <term>sublimating</term>, <term>calcinating</term>, <term>dissolving</term>, and <term>coagulating</term> <note>These are the core laboratory processes of alchemy: purifying through heat, evaporation, burning to ash, liquidizing, and solidifying.</note>, with such frequent repetition until the impurity that the matter contained is driven out and it becomes natural and lovely. In this manner you should understand what I mean when I speak of a thing from which you should remove what is in excess and give it what it lacks. This is my opinion and that of all <term>Philosophers</term> <note>The "Philosophers" refers to the long lineage of alchemists who sought the secrets of nature.</note>; when they think on this, they wish it to be meant and understood just as I have given you sufficient instruction.

<meta>catchword: CA</meta>

<summary>This page explains the practical purification of plant matter, instructing the reader to remove impure oils and use alchemical processes like distillation and calcination to balance the substance's qualities. It emphasizes the philosophical principle of removing "excess" and restoring "lack" to achieve a perfected state.</summary>

### CHAPTER XXIII.

Now let us return to our first work, namely how we should join the *body* <note>original: "Corpus"</note> and *spirit* <note>original: "Spiritum"</note> together: Once you have thus divided the spirit and body from each other, you should skim off the oil with a feather so that nothing remains upon [the surface]. You should put the oil into a glass vessel, sealed tightly with the <term>Lute of Wisdom</term> <note>original: "Luto Sapientiæ"; a specialized clay or paste used by alchemists to hermetically seal glass vessels and prevent the escape of precious vapors.</note>, and keep it carefully. For you will perform wonders with it once it is prepared, as I shall teach you how to prepare it hereafter. But the unprepared oil is currently unnatural and poisonous, for the poisonous heat that the herbs once held remains within it. This heat must be driven out; then you will afterwards perform miraculous cures with it. 

Once you have removed the oil, put the spirit into a <term>flask</term> <note>original: "Kolben"; the bottom vessel of a distillation setup, sometimes called a cucurbit.</note>, place a <term>head</term> <note>original: "Helm"; the alembic or still-head that collects the rising steam.</note> upon it, and attach a <term>receiver</term> <note>original: "Vorlage"</note> to it. Distill it over with a tempered fire—neither too hot nor too cold. When you have drawn it over, add the *dregs* <note>original: "feces"</note> remaining in the flask to the <term>earth</term> <note>The solid plant matter remaining after processing.</note> so they may be *calcined* <note>original: "calciniren"; a process of heating the matter to a high temperature to reduce it to a purified white ash.</note> with it. Put the *spirit* back into the flask, place the head upon it and the *receiver* <note>original: "receptacul"</note> onto it, and distill it over as you did the first time. You shall *distill* it in this manner ten times, over and over again. 

By this process, the spirit becomes lovely, sweet, and natural, which before was poisonous, harsh, evil, sharp, and of no use to any human. Now, however, it is lovely, good, and natural, so much so that its virtue cannot be described or spoken of. This is the fatty spirit which I 
mean—

<summary>This chapter provides instructions for the rectification of the 'fatty spirit' through ten cycles of distillation and the separate storage of the plant's essential oil. Hollandus emphasizes that the spirit must be purified from its initial 'poisonous' state to become a sweet and medically potent substance.</summary>

<keywords>distillation, fatty spirit, essential oil, Lute of Wisdom, calcination, purification, rectification, alchemy, pharmaceutical preparation</keywords>

### CHAPTER XXIV.

Now we also wish to report concerning the Earth or Body; the same must be prepared with great skill. For when the **Element** <note>original: "Element"</note> of the Earth is **prepared** <note>original: "præparirt"</note>, and brought to its first **Nature** <note>original: "Natur"</note> and to its own essence, it performs such wonderful things that its power is indescribable. This is because the Earth, which was previously black and useless, caused all elements to desire to flee and separate from it, because they were clothed and covered with an earthly blackness. Consequently, the **Elements** <note>original: "Elementa"</note> could not exercise their **Nature** <note>original: "Natur"</note>. 

But now, after the Earth has been brought to its highest power—namely, into its own essence—it reaches the state in which it first existed when it was created by GOD, before **Adam** and **Eve**, and before GOD subsequently cursed the four elements so that they became corruptible and corrupted. And so, if you have brought the Earth to its purity, the elements will now desire to be with it, though they fled from it before, as I have taught previously. 

Therefore, when you have drawn all elements out of the Earth, place the Earth into a long earthen vessel made of fired potter's clay, so that it may be better heated through. Set it in the furnace of **calcination** <note>original: "Calcination"; the process of heating a substance to high temperatures in the presence of air to remove volatile impurities and reduce it to a powder.</note>, and **calcine** <note>original: "calciniret"</note> it for three days and nights in as much glow and strong fire as you can provide; and when the three

Once purified by three days of fire, the solid matter can once again harmonize with the volatile elements of the plant.</summary>

Now one shall learn another way of distilling herbs, from which the water passes over first; hereby one will understand all types of herbs from which the **Water of the Clouds** <note>original: *Wasser der Wolcken*; this refers to the first clear, watery distillate that rises during the process, often thought to carry the plant's "spirit."</note> first ascends: for by this operation and the teaching contained within it, one understands everything that is to be made from green herbs and roots; afterward, instruction will be given regarding all dry **spices** <term>Specien</term>, gums, woods, and everything that is dry / each with its own instruction.

# Vegetable Work.

[...and the third herb] has already been under the snow the whole winter / yet it does not perish / and thus it yields neither heat nor cold / dryness nor moisture; it is the very best and most powerful among the three herbs / yet little esteemed / because it is found in such abundance. I tell you / **GOD** has infused such an influence <note>The author uses the word "Influentz" to mean a divine or celestial power flowing into the plant.</note> into this herb / that it cannot be sufficiently described by any human: Therefore / my child / we wish to extract the second **Vegetable Stone** <term>Vegetabel Stein</term> <note>An alchemical substance derived from plants, believed to have healing or transmutative properties, similar to the Philosopher's Stone.</note> from it / to cure <note>original: "curiren"</note> all people of all sicknesses / and to sustain them in a healthy state until the final hour of their lives; as well as to congeal <note>original: "congeliren"; in alchemy, this refers to turning a liquid substance into a solid or fixed state.</note> **mercury** <term>☿</term> into fine **gold** <term>☉</term>. <meta>This page continues the instructions for processing Celandine. The previous page ended with the instruction to clean the herb and place it in jugs for distillation, taking care not to crush it. This page explains the philosophical and alchemical reasons why crushing the plant is destructive to its medicinal potency.</meta>

### Johann Isaac Hollandus

...if one were to pound the herbs, a part of the three spirits would flee—namely the greenness or color, a part of its "air" or flavor, and a part of its natural warmth. For these three spirits are so volatile <note>original: "flüchtig"; referring to substances that easily evaporate or escape into the air.</note> that they cannot endure any pounding or crushing. Because of this, you would mostly lose them, and your work would thereafter be ruined; for you would only be treating a dead **body** <term>Corpus</term> <note>In alchemy, the "body" refers to the physical matter or salt left behind once the vital "spirits" or "soul" have been driven out.</note> that has been robbed of its soul and life, because the herb is **mortified** <term>mortificiret</term> <note>To "mortify" a substance means to kill its natural life-force or destroy its organic structure, rendered it "dead" in an alchemical sense.</note> through the pounding. 

Test it for yourself: pound a green herb very small in a **mortar** <term>Mörsel</term>; it will quickly lose its green color and natural moisture, as the entire house where it is pounded will be filled with the scent of the herb. Yet that scent no longer grows once the nature of the herb has been broken off and is mortified, so that it is, as it were, estranged from the nature and **influence** <term>Influentz</term> <note>The author refers to the Neo-Platonic idea that celestial powers from the stars "flow" into plants to give them their specific virtues.</note> of the heavens, which causes its scent to grow. Furthermore, the heavens and the stars, which cast or give their influence into it, provide no more help once it is broken off, so that it no longer has assistance from any side. 

For this reason, at that very hour, the three volatile spirits—which are its life, its soul, and its **quintessence** <term>quinta Essentia</term> <note>The "fifth essence" or the most purified, spiritualized core of a substance, believed to be the source of its healing power.</note>—depart from it. Thus, herbs can no more endure pounding or crushing than a human being can endure being hacked into pieces; for the soul, which is his life, would depart from him in that very instant. Therefore, my child, do not pound green herbs, but do as you were instructed above,

<summary>Johann Isaac Hollandus explains that crushing green herbs kills their "life force" and causes their vital spirits and quintessence to evaporate. He uses the analogy of a human soul leaving a body to warn the alchemist against using a mortar, which would leave only a "dead" physical substance behind.</summary>

### CHAPTER V.

Now we proceed to our work. All that which remained, you shall grind on a stone and put it together into a large jug; place the same into a lukewarm **water bath** <term>Balneum</term> <note>original: "Balneum"; a vessel of warm water used to provide steady, gentle heat to an alchemical flask, preventing the ingredients from burning.</note>, pour its own water upon it, stir it well together with a wooden spoon, then place a polished glass lid over the mouth. Let it stand thus for two days and nights; stir it well every four or five hours so that the water may thoroughly extract the **Elements** <term>Elementa</term> <note>In alchemical theory, these are the four building blocks—Fire, Air, Water, and Earth—that make up the plant's medicinal virtue.</note>.

At the end of the two days, take out the jug, set it aside, and let it settle for three or four days. Then pour the clear liquid off from the **dregs** <term>fecibus</term> <note>original: "fecibus"; the solid sediment or waste left over after a liquid has been poured off.</note> into a clean jug and filter it. Put the liquid into another jug; it is a **Golden Water** <term>Gülden Wasser</term> <note>A potent extract, likely named for its amber or yellow color, containing the "soul" or active essence of the herb.</note>. Cork it and protect it well.

Then pour other water upon the **dregs** <term>feces</term> again and stir it well; it would also be good to make them very dry before pouring the water on them, etc. Place it back into the **water bath** <term>Balneum</term> for two days and two nights; stir it as before, cover it, etc. Afterward, let it cool and do as before: pour this water in with the first batch, take more water again, and pour it upon the dregs. Do this as often as necessary until the dregs no longer give any color to the water. Then you have extracted the Air and the Fire out of the Earth, and the pouring is [finished]. Should it however happen that you do not [have enough] water...

## CHAPTER VI.

Now, my child <note>Alchemical texts were often written as instructions from a master to a student, addressed affectionately as "child" or "son."</note>, we shall return to our work to **rectify** <term>rectificiren</term> <note>To purify a liquid by repeated distillation or filtration to reach a higher state of potency.</note> our **Air** and **Fire** <note>These represent the volatile and most active fractions of the plant's essence, often associated with the spirit and the oil.</note> together once more and clean them of their **dregs** <term>fecibus</term>. Put all the colored water into a clean bowl; then take the whites of 40 or 50 eggs and beat them until they are as thin as water with a wooden spoon. Pour them into the colored water and beat them for half an hour so that they mix well. Then place the kettle over the fire; let it slowly become hot and finally boil, but do not stir it at all. Then remove it from the fire. Have a large, white, woolen **Hippocras bag** <term>Ypocras-Beutel</term> <note>original: "Ypocras-Beutel"; a conical fabric filter used since the Middle Ages for straining and clarifying liquids.</note> at hand. Pour all your water into it and let it strain through into a glass pot. When it no longer drips, take distilled water and pour it into the bag over the curdled egg whites. Let it sink through the **dregs** <term>feces</term> to [wash] the Elements out of the dregs...

<header>Vegetable Work.</header>

...[to] draw out; do this as long as the **dregs** <term>feces</term> <note>original: "feces"; the solid sediment or waste left over after a liquid has been poured off or filtered.</note> give any color to the water; then you have drawn all the **Elements** <note>The four fundamental building blocks (Fire, Air, Water, and Earth) believed to constitute the medicinal essence of the plant.</note> out of it. Dry the dregs in a pan and preserve them; they must be put back with the Earth into the **retort** <term>Retort</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-curving neck used for distilling substances through heat.</note> in order to draw out the combustible oil with the **sal ammoniac** <term>Salarmeniac</term> <note>original: "Salarmeniac"; ammonium chloride, often used by alchemists to help "open" a substance or assist in the extraction of oils.</note>, for there are many dregs in the white of eggs.

Following this, take the liquid that has leaked from the bag, place it in the **water bath** <term>Balneum</term> <note>original: "Balneum"; a method of heating a vessel by placing it in warm water to ensure a gentle, steady temperature.</note>, and distill it until it is so dry that it turns to dust. Then pour the water back onto it, stir it well, and let it stand for 24 hours in the warm water bath. Stir it occasionally with a wooden spoon and cover the mouth of the vessel again with a piece of polished glass. Then take it out and let it sit for 3 or 4 days; then tilt it gently on its side and pour it off carefully through a **filter** <note>original: "per filtrum".</note>. See whether you find any dregs at the bottom; if not, it is sufficiently clarified. However, if you find dregs, it is not clear, but must be **clarified** <term>geclarificirt</term> again as before.

### CHAPTER VII.

My child shall know that of all things in the world, if one draws off their water via **distillation** <term>distillationẽ</term> until dry, so that it turns to dust, and then pounds and sieves it, and afterwards grinds it small upon a stone and pours the water back onto it that was drawn from it—or other common distilled water—and one places it in the water bath, then the water draws into itself all the **Elemental** Water, Air, and Fire, and it becomes red; and the redness lies in the innermost part of the **greenness** <note>In alchemical philosophy, the "greenness" (*viriditas*) is the raw life force of the plant, while the "redness" represents the hidden, perfected spirit or "soul" that is extracted through the work.</note>.

### CHAPTER IX. <note>original: "CAPUT IX"</note>

**I**n such a way, my child, must the Element Fire, the Elemental Water, and the Elemental Air—along with the three spirits—all six be drawn out of the earth <note>The "earth" refers to the solid plant matter or the "feces" left after extraction.</note> into one mass, without distillation. One can draw them out of the earth in no other way in the world, for the three spirits previously mentioned rest in natural warmth and heat—namely taste, tincture <note>In this context, tincture refers to the concentrated color or the medicinal essence.</note>, and smell. These three cannot tolerate any heat that is applied with fire; for if one were to draw Water, Air, and Fire out of the earth through distillation, it would have to be done with hot dryness from the fire, <gloss>as</gloss> from ashes or sand. Through the water-bath <term>Balneum</term>, the Elemental Water, Air, and Fire <gloss>would</gloss> not rise; instead, only the "water of the clouds" <note>Simple moisture or common water, lacking the spiritual qualities of the plant.</note> would rise. If one then wished to force the Elemental Water, Air, and Fire over through distillation, it would have to be done with direct fire; yet, as said, the three aforementioned spirits [cannot tolerate] the heat...

...cannot endure the fire, which is applied with fire; they would invisibly fly away, and then you would be rid of their life and soul and their Quintessence <term>Quinta Essentia</term> <note>The "fifth essence" or the purest, most concentrated medicinal core of a substance, beyond the four physical elements.</note>, and you would have a dead body <term>Corpus</term> <note>In alchemy, the "body" is the solid, material remains of a substance after its "spirit" or "soul" has been removed.</note>. Even though you would still have the four elements <term>Elementen</term> together, they would be bereft of their soul and Quintessence, which hold and bind the four elements together. For once these three spirits have departed, the four elements cannot remain together but must separate from one another and begin to rot and perish. Each element returns to its own nature: air to air, fire to fire, water to water, and earth to earth, etc.

Take for example a person who has died and the natural warmth is gone; immediately the color that resided in the blood, his natural smell, and his taste <note>original: "Schmack"; meaning the vital flavor or sensory essence.</note>—all three have departed from the person. These three are the soul that hold the corpse together in one being. Understand well, my child, what this is: when a child is conceived in its mother’s womb through natural means, then within forty days a human being is formed from it. All limbs are prepared through the warmth of nature <margin>which</margin> the mother has in her blood. For in the blood <margin>rest the</margin> three spirits, as if in natural warmth: <margin>namely the</margin> warmth, the smell, and the taste. # Vegetabel-Werck. #

...in the blood of the woman; and the child's limbs are formed with the help of nature. At that time, these three spirits are present in all such forms and limbs of the child, but only in very small amounts. For the tiny limbs of a child, when its parts are first prepared—namely within forty days—are initially so tender and small, as if they were little wires, and the child is like a tiny grain of seed. Therefore, there cannot be much of these three spirits within them. 

And as soon as the tiny limbs are formed, God sends the soul into them, which comes from His most high will, springing forth quite miraculously <note>original: *miraculose*</note>, of which nothing more may be said here, because it does not belong to this subject matter <note>original: *materia*</note>. The soul has an eternal essence, without beginning in GOD; therefore it comes from GOD. Even though all the tiny limbs are formed so small, the soul arises and surges <note>The original German "wallet" suggests a bubbling or active movement of life force.</note> from that hour on in the body <term>Leichnam</term> <note>While *Leichnam* means "corpse" in modern German, in this period it often referred to the physical frame or "hulk" of a living being.</note>. 

For if the soul did not enter it from that hour on, the three spirits would depart from it. Therefore, all three spirits must first be in the human being before the soul surges within, and the three spirits hold the soul together in the body. As long as these three spirits are with the body, the soul also remains; and as the body becomes larger, older, and stronger, these three spirits gradually become larger, older, and stronger. For this reason, they are called **growing spirits**. And as quickly as these three spirits leave the body <term>Corpus</term> <note>Latin for body; used here to denote the material substance.</note>, the soul must follow at once and vacate the physical frame. For it has no stead or place where it may rest. Test this on

The "corpse" is the de-animated physical matter left behind after the "soul" or "spirit" has been extracted.</note> / and have sunk to the bottom of the glass. # THE FIFTH ESSENCE
## to be extracted from Sugar.
### CHAPTER 1.

Now I will teach my child a great secret: how one should prepare the **fifth essence** <term>Quinta Essentia</term> <note>The "fifth essence" or ether was considered by alchemists to be a pure, incorruptible substance—the "spirit" of a thing—that existed beyond the four physical elements of earth, air, fire, and water.</note> from **sugar**, which holds the prize above all **Vegetable Works** <note>Alchemical processes were often categorized into the "Animal," "Mineral," or "Vegetable" (plant-based) works.</note> because of its **temperament** <note>original: *temperatur*. In historical medicine and alchemy, this refers to the specific balance of the four primary qualities: hot, cold, moist, and dry.</note>, which resembles the incorruptible Heaven. For Heaven is neither hot nor cold, nor dry nor moist, but is of the most tempered or entirely equal harmony. And yet it is composed of the four elements; however, they are not in conflict with one another, but are so unified and balanced that they may never again be separated, but shall remain harmoniously simple and **fixed** <note>A "fixed" substance is stable and resistant to the transformative power of fire; it does not evaporate or change state easily.</note> for eternity. Now, the Earth receives moisture, warmth, and whatever it requires from Heaven, even though Heaven itself is neither warm nor cold, nor wet nor dry. It is exactly the same with the fifth essence of sugar; like gold, it has the four elements fixed within it, except that gold is pure while sugar is impure. Gold is warm and moist on the outside, but cold, dry, and white on the inside; sugar, however, is the opposite, for it is cold and dry on the outside, from with-

<meta>catchword: in</meta>

<summary>Hollandus begins instructions for the "Vegetable Work," explaining that sugar contains a perfectly balanced "Fifth Essence" similar to gold and the celestial heavens. He describes this state as a harmonious union of the four elements that cannot be separated once purified.</summary>
<keywords>Fifth Essence, Quinta Essentia, sugar, Vegetable Work, alchemy, four elements, gold, temperament, Johannes Isaac Hollandus</keywords>

->Vegetabel-Werck<-

is, however, warm and moist, and at the same time red and **fixed** <note>In alchemy, a "fixed" substance is stable and does not evaporate or change when exposed to fire.</note>, both inside and out. It lacks nothing except that its interior should be turned outward, so that its redness stands on the outside and the **dregs** <term>feces</term> <note>Latin: *feces*. The impure, solid remains or sediment left after a process like distillation or filtration.</note> are separated from it. Then it is prepared; for it does not need to be fixed, as it is already fixed from within and without, and it retains all volatile spirits and whatever else is volatile within itself.

Now I wish to show its nature and where it comes from, namely from a **cane-juice** <note>original: *Rohrschuff*. This refers to the juice of the sugar cane.</note>. However, if you desire more complete instruction on this, read the 33rd chapter on the generation of things that grow in the sea and other waters, where we treat its nature extensively; here it shall suffice to show in what manner it is prepared and in what way it may be used in **medicine** <note>original: *Medicin*.</note>. Its nature is such that it tends to hold all volatile spirits and fix them into a stone, as shall be explained in what follows. First, it must be known that the primary **dregs** or yeasts cannot be separated from it until its inner essence is brought out—namely, so that its color appears as a dark golden hue. And when it has been **distilled** <note>original: *distilliret*. The laboratory process of heating a substance to create vapor and then condensing it into liquid to purify or separate its components.</note> in this way, one then sees its redness and fire through the yellowness of its air and combustible oil. Only then can you separate the **dregs** from the **fifth essence** <note>original: *fünfften Wesen*. The "Fifth Essence" or Quintessence was believed to be the purest, most medicinal part of a substance, representing its celestial or spiritual core.</note>. The preparation of this **Fifth Essence** <term>Quintæ Essentiæ</term> is as follows:

## CHAPTER II.

Take the very best sugar from the island of **Madeira** <note>original: *Madeva*. A Portuguese archipelago famous for its sugar production during the Renaissance.</note>, which should be very white and hard. You do not need to **dissolve** <term>solviren</term> and **solidify** <term>congeliren</term> it many times; for even if some impurity is present, it does not prevent its internal essence from being brought out. Then, once its inner redness emerges, it must be **purified** <term>purgiren</term> <note>To cleanse the substance of its remaining physical "dregs" or impurities.</note>.

Take about 10 or 12 pounds of sugar—more or less, as you see fit—and grind it into a fine powder. Place it in a strong earthen jug or into two or three large glass vessels. Pour **Water of Life** <term>Aquam vitæ</term> <note>original: *Aquam vitæ*. High-proof distilled alcohol, often used as a solvent to extract the "soul" or essence of a substance.</note>—which has been **rectified** <note>Purified and strengthened through repeated distillation.</note> two or three times—over it, so that the liquid stands eight or nine finger-breadths above the sugar. Place a **still-head** <term>Helm</term> <note>original: *Helm*. The cap of an alembic or distilling apparatus where vapors collect and condense.</note> on top and distill it in a boiling **water bath** <term>Balneo</term> <note>original: *Balneo* or *Balneum Mariae*. A vessel of boiling water used to heat the glass gently and evenly.</note>. Let the bath boil thoroughly so that everything rises. When no more liquid remains [in the bottom vessel], let it cool, and pour the distilled liquid back onto the sugar. Distill it off once more, and repeat this process six or seven times.

After this, let it cool, open the vessel, and take out the sugar. Place it in a glass that can withstand fire and set it upon sifted **ashes** <note>An ash bath, used in the laboratory to provide a steady, dry heat and to protect the glass from the direct flame.</note>. Pour the distilled liquid over it once again and distill off half of it—not much more, so that the sugar does not burn. When you have distilled half of it away, warm the liquid slightly (so that the glass does not crack, as it might if you poured it in cold) and pour it back in. The still-head should have a hole at the top so that it can be poured back through a **funnel** <term>Trechter</term>...

...can be poured back in; for you must repeat this often and maintain a strong fire, so that the **water bath** <term>Balneum</term> <note>original: *Balneum*. A vessel of boiling water used for gentle, even heating.</note> causes the sugar in the glass to boil. Every time you have distilled over half of the **Water of Life** <term>Aqua Vitæ</term> <note>High-proof distilled alcohol used as a solvent.</note>, pour it back on immediately. If you were to let all of it evaporate, the sugar would burn, as it must always be kept in a boiling state; otherwise, it would become scorched, for the combustible oiliness is still within the sugar and burns easily. Therefore, you must always draw off only half, then warm the distillate and pour it back on. Continue this cycle of pouring on and distilling until you see through the glass that the sugar has turned blood-red. This happens in about ten or twelve days, provided you work well and maintain the proper fire.

## CHAPTER III.

When you see that it has turned red, let it cool and remove it from the cupel <note>original: *Test*. A shallow, porous vessel used in heating processes, often nested in ashes.</note> with the ashes. When absolutely nothing more distills, keep it for four or five more days in the boiling **water bath** <term>Balneo</term>, so that the sugar may properly **solidify** <term>coagulire</term> <note>original: *coagulire*. The process of thickening or hardening a substance.</note>. Then let it cool, break open the vessel, and take out the material, which will be pitch-black once it has **solidified** <term>congelirt</term> <note>original: *congelirt*. To set or "freeze" into a solid state.</note>. **Pulverize** it; then take a well-fired stone **cucurbit** <term>Cucurbit</term> <note>original: *Cucurbit*. A gourd-shaped vessel used as the base of a distilling apparatus.</note>, put this sugar into it—or into two or three flasks—and pour over it a considerable quantity...

nothing will remain, no differently than if you had let 
another oil or fat burn; for whatever would remain after 
such [a burning], just as much would this sugar 
leave behind.

## CHAPTER IV.

Now take all the red-colored water together; place it in the **distillation flask** <term>cucurbit</term> <note>original: *cucurbit*. A gourd-shaped vessel that forms the lower part of a distilling apparatus.</note> into the **water bath** <term>Balneum</term> <note>original: *Balneum*. A container of hot water used to provide gentle, even heat to the vessel placed within it.</note>, and draw it off, or let it **evaporate** <term>evaporiren</term>, provided you have enough distilled water in stock. And when it has smoked off until dry, let it cool; take it out, remove the **still-head** <note>The "Helm" or alembic cap that fits onto the distillation flask.</note>, and pour other distilled water upon it again. Stir it well together with a wooden spoon and keep it again in the **water bath** <term>Baln.</term>. Then let it settle; pour the clear liquid from the **sediment** <note>original: *Hefen*. Literally "yeast," but here referring to the cloudy dregs.</note> as before, and do this until the sugar leaves absolutely no more **dregs** <term>feces</term> <note>original: *feces*. The insoluble waste or solid residue remaining at the bottom.</note> behind. Then set it to **evaporate** <term>evaporiren</term> until a skin forms on top. When that happens, place it in a cool place for eight or ten days; then it will crystallize into little red stones, just like rubies or other **Philosophical stones** <note>In alchemy, these are crystalline substances produced through laboratory processes, believed to hold concentrated medicinal or transformative power.</note>. But should it not crystallize within those ten days, then the water in the **flask** <term>cucurbit</term> has not yet been sufficiently steamed away, and you must let more of it **evaporate** <note>original: *exhaliren* (exhale).</note>; then it will **turn to stone** <term>lapilliren</term> <note>original: *lapilliren*. From the Latin *lapillus*, meaning to form into small stones or crystals.</note>. However, if you do not want it in the form of little stones but rather as a powder, then let it all evaporate gradually in a warm place until a dark yellow powder remains, like gold; then the **Medicine** <term>Medicin</term> is prepared. Thus you have the **fixed Fifth Essence** <term>Quintam Essentiam fixam</term> <note>original: *Quintam Essentiam fixam*. # To extract the **Fifth Essence** <term>Quinta Essentia</term> <note>original: *Quintam Essentiam*. The pure, spiritualized core of a substance, believed to possess ultimate medicinal power.</note> from the herb **Sundew** <note>original: *Solaria*, or *Ros Solis*, which is called *Sonnen-thau*.</note>.

## CHAPTER I.

Now I wish to teach my child the most hidden secret of **vegetable** <note>original: *Vegetabilischen*. Pertaining to the kingdom of plants.</note> things, the power and virtue of which all the old masters have kept hidden. One master has sworn and bound another by oath that they should not make the power of this "solar herb" known in any books or writings that they might publish. Therefore, no one can attain the potency of this herb who does not already possess the entire **Vegetable Work** <term>Opus Vegetabile</term> <note>original: *Opus Vegetabile*. The alchemical process of purifying and transforming plant matter.</note>; for in that same **Vegetable Work** is contained the power of all herbs, stones, and resins, and everything that is **medicinal**. Such a **Vegetable Work** comes into the hands of no one except the sworn masters of the old philosophers, and those who are knowledgeable in the **Liberal Arts** <note>The traditional seven branches of knowledge: grammar, logic, rhetoric, arithmetic, geometry, music, and astronomy.</note>. Thus, no one has accomplished it except Master **Isaac** and his son, and now finally my child. Therefore, my son, I charge you by the living GOD and your inheritance of the Kingdom of Heaven, that you keep this art and its power secret.

## CHAPTER II.

# Vegetable Work

...collect the entire herb including the roots, leaves, and flowers with caution, so that no earth, filth, or other kinds of weeds remain with it; clean it of its earth and impurities as much as possible, for you must not wash the herb with water or introduce any other moisture. For this reason, you must not collect it when the weather is rainy or darkly damp, but rather at the hottest time, at noon.

Now cut the herb small, pound and grind it well in a marble, wooden, or iron **mortar**, but in no case a copper one, and put it quickly into a stone jar or flask, or into vial-glasses, well-sealed with their corresponding **helms** <term>Helm (Still-head): The upper part of a distilling apparatus that directs rising vapors into a cooling tube or receiver.</term>. Place it in the **Balneum** <term>Balneum (Water bath): Also known as a "bain-marie," this is a vessel of boiling water used to provide gentle, even heat to the distillation flask.</term>, alongside the attached **receiver** <term>Vorlage (Receiver): The glass vessel used to catch and collect the liquid that has been distilled.</term>, and distill all the water over in the boiling **water bath** until nothing more will pass. Air and water will pass over together, and the water will be yellow like the finest **gold** <note>original: *☉*, the alchemical symbol for Gold and the Sun.</note>; no other herb does this except for **Sundew** <note>original: *Sonnentau*</note>.

After it no longer drips, let it remain in the **water bath** for another three or four days, and keep it boiling day and night, so that all moisture leaves it and the **substance** <note>original: *materia*</note> becomes so dry that it turns to dust inside the glass. Then let it cool, remove the **receiver** <note>original: *receptacul*</note> in which the water is held, and plug it well. Afterward, remove the **helm** from the distillation vessel and take the herb; grind it on a stone to a subtle powder so that it may be sieved through a narrow sieve. FURTHERMORE, you should know that within the powder and **dregs** <note>original: *fecibus*</note> there is still a combustible oil, and if you wish, you can extract it **by descent** <note>original: *per descensum*; a method where heat is applied from above to force the essence of a substance downward into a receiver</note>, for it is good against cold **gout** <note>original: *podagra*</note>, for stiffened limbs, and if the nerves have perhaps become slack. The Quintessence was believed to be a "fifth element" that contained none of these imbalances.</note> could arise in that person's body / as is taught in the 36th Chapter concerning <term>vegetables|original: *Vegetabilien*; here referring to the plant kingdom</term> / therein the <term>Quintessence</term> of all medicinal things is discussed / where it is also proven with God's help / that human life can be protected from all sickness and prolonged / until that same final day which is appointed for him: So that a person may always be maintained in such a state as one is accustomed to be at thirty years of age / in strength and power of understanding. # How one should prepare the Quintessence from Honey.

## CHAPTER I.

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'J' with floral and scrollwork patterns.</image-desc>
Now, this time I want to make known to you a great **secret** <note>original: *Secret*; a hidden alchemical mystery or specialized knowledge</note> in the **Vegetable Work** <term>Vegetable Work: The branch of alchemy focused on the extraction of medicinal essences from plants</term>, namely the wondrous nature of honey, which is the most subtle and most noble of all herbs and flowers, from whose purer and nobler part it is drawn out by the little bees. For the bees are so natured that they draw the best from them, as is extensively reported in the **Animal Work** <term>Animal Work: The branch of alchemy dealing with substances of animal origin</term>, and there instruction is given on how to extract the natures of animals, and this is described primarily in the 84th Chapter there. 

My child should know that everything God has created is extremely good, perfect, and incorruptible, like the heavens; but all things to be found here below—such as animals, fish, and whatever has a sensitive life, as well as herbs, plants, and whatever it may ever be—have a twofold nature, namely a perfect and an imperfect one; the perfect is called the **Quintessence** <note>original Latin: *Quinta Essentia*; the "fifth essence," the purest and most potent medicinal core of a substance</note>, but the imperfect consists of the **dregs** <note>original Latin: *feces*; the waste matter or sediment</note> or the poisonous, combustible oil. You shall separate such dregs and combustible oil, and what then remains is perfect, and is called the **Quintessence**, which lasts forever

For when God created all things and looked upon them/ then everything was perfect and good/ and nothing was lacking in any way: This I tell you out of love / that God has placed into all created things a secret nature or influence <note>original: *Influentz*; here referring to a celestial or spiritual power flowing from the stars into earthly matter</note>/ and to the whole of nature He has given a general influence/ and also to every certain kind or species He gave its own particular influence or virtue <note>original: *Tugend*; the inherent medicinal power or "strength" of a substance</note>/ either in medicine or other secret effects / which in part come to light through natural art/ but are by far even more hidden than what is known to our senses. Do you believe that an herb which is held to be determined for this or that sickness/ does not contain more powers / than those that are known to us? In truth, many more. For if God has poured the power to heal into other things, what then will be found in honey, which is drawn from almost countless flowers, since every herb has its own gift? In truth, if one brings it to its highest state, it will **operate** <note>original Latin: *operiren*; to act upon the body or produce a medicinal effect</note> miraculously. Therefore, take heed of what lies hidden in this <term>Quintessence <note>original Latin: *Quinta Essentia*; literally the "fifth essence," the purest and most concentrated medicinal core of a substance, separated from its physical impurities</note></term>, so that you do not esteem it lightly, but rather keep it secret as the most excellent part of the entire **Animal Work** <note>In alchemy, the "Animal Work" refers to the preparation of medicines derived from animal sources—such as blood, urine, or honey—rather than metals or plants</note>; and if you possess such a thing, you need nothing else for the removal of every evil affliction of the body.

### CHAPTER II.

Now I will proceed to the practice. Take twelve quarts of the best fresh virgin honey <note>original: *Jungfern-Honigs*; this refers to the purest honey that flows naturally from the honeycomb without being pressed</note>, put it into a large stone jar, and place a helm <note>a still-head or cap used in distillation</note> upon it, luted <note>original: *lutirt*; sealed airtight using a paste made of clay, flour, or lime</note> twice. Place it in the water bath <term>Balneum <gloss>a "bath of Mary" or double-boiler used for gentle, even heating</gloss></term>, and lute a receiver <note>original: *Vorlage*; the vessel that catches the distilled liquid</note> to it. Let the water bath boil, and **distill for as long as anything will rise.** My child, know that in honey there is no "water of the clouds" <note>common rainwater or atmospheric moisture</note>, but **only the Philosophical Elemental Water.** The element of Air goes over first along with the element of Fire, in which the Air is enclosed. This Air tastes like distilled <term>Water of Life <gloss>original Latin: *Aqua vitæ*; usually referring to concentrated alcohol or brandy</gloss></term> and rises first from the honey; in both appearance and taste, it cannot be distinguished from a <term>Water of Life</term>. Continue with the distillation until nothing more passes over. After this, let the jar, along with the helm and receiver, stand for about 15 days in the <term>water bath</term>, and keep the <term>water bath</term> constantly boiling so that the matter <note>original Latin: *materia*</note> becomes entirely dry; for the distillation of honey in a <term>water bath</term> is very difficult because of its tough viscosity <note>original: *zehen Feiſte*; literally "tough fatness," referring to the thick, sticky nature of the honey</note>, and one must let it stand therein for a good 15 or 20 days before it can be made completely dry. Then let it cool and take it out; detach the receiver and the helm, and pour what is in the receiver back onto the dry matter. Place it back into the <term>water bath</term>, and fit a stopper or ground glass into the mouth of the jar so that it closes well, and lute it in the best possible way; but let the <term>water bath</term> be only lukewarm. In this manner, my child, one might (and even if it were

### CHAPTER III.

When you have, as indicated above, drawn off all the **Air** and made the remaining matter <note>original: *matery*</note> entirely dry, then remove the receiver <term>recipient <gloss>original: *recipienten*; the vessel that receives the distilled liquid</gloss></term> and plug it carefully. Then take rain-water that has been distilled two or three times; pour a good quantity of it—as much as seems sufficient to you—into the jug. Cover it, yet leave it unluted <note>not sealed airtight</note>, and keep it in a boiling <term>water bath <gloss>original Latin: *Balneo*</gloss></term> for three days and nights. Every day, stir it four or five times with a wooden spoon. Afterward, let it cool, take it out, and let the solids settle. Then take another clean jug and pour the clear liquid into it; upon the <term>dregs <gloss>original Latin: *feces*; the solid waste or sediment left after distillation or infusion</gloss></term>, pour more good distilled water as before. Place the jug back into the <term>water bath <gloss>original Latin: *Balneum*</gloss></term>, take it out again afterward, and let it settle. Pour off the clear liquid and add a good portion of other distilled water again, just as before; and do this as often—

...the clear portion from it to that which was previously poured off; repeat such pouring on and off for the third time / when that is done / then throw the <term>sediment <gloss>Latin: *feces* — the solid dregs remaining after a liquid is strained or settled</gloss></term> away / they are of no more use. After that, take the pot / in which all the combined water is / place it in the boiling <term>water bath <gloss>Latin: *balneum* — a vessel of hot water used to heat another container gently</gloss></term>, let it distill off until it is completely dry / and then let it become cold / then pour the water back over such Earth or Salt / and keep it in the boiling water bath to <term>dissolve <gloss>Latin: *solviren*</gloss></term> for one day / after that let it cool and settle / then pour the clear part of it into another clean pot / and upon the sediment again a little distilled water / set it for three or four hours in the hot water bath, take it out again / and let it settle for a few hours / then pour the clear part off gently from the top to join the first portion / throw the sediment away / there is nothing more left in it. After this, set the pot with the Earth or Salt back into the water bath, draw the water off until completely dry / and proceed in every way as has been said. Repeat this operation as often / until no more sediment remains at the bottom. And when you draw the water from the Earth / then you will find it as beautiful and clear as a crystal, and thus you have your element pure.

# On the Inner, Hidden, and Transitory Essence of the Four Elements.

---

The first of all things created by God the Lord was Water, into which God incorporated His own Earth; and everything that is endowed with essence and life has its origin from the Earth. Thus, there are two manifest Elements, namely Water and Earth, in which the remaining two are hidden—namely, Fire in the Earth, and Air in the Water—and they are bound together in such a way that they may not be divided. Two of them are fixed, namely Earth and Fire, and two are volatile <note>original: "flüchtige"</note>, namely Water and Air.

<summary>This page concludes the medicinal instructions for honey-based "Fifth Essence" and begins a new section explaining the alchemical cosmology of the four elements.</summary>
<keywords>medicine, honey, projection, Mineral Stone, four elements, creation, fixed vs volatile, John Isaac Hollandus</keywords>
