<unclear>al</unclear>so each of them has a part of the property of the others. Beyond this, there are two elements contained within every single element / namely a destructible-decaying-and-combustible one / and also an eternal and indestructible one like the Heavens. Besides these, there are still hidden in these natures / that which one calls the stinking *Feces* <gloss>Latin for dregs or waste; in alchemy, this refers to the "caput mortuum," the useless material left after distillation.</gloss> / which also rob the others of their power and hinder them / so that they have little or nothing / and thereby the other *elements* <note>original: "Elementa"</note> are made stinking / and subject to decay. There are also two kinds of Water / such as the elemental / and secondly the rain- or river-water / just as the Earth is of two kinds / one clear / luminous / white / elemental; the other black / stinking and combustible; of Fire there are likewise two types / one Elemental - Natural - eternal / and for the other a stinking-combustible one; And thus it is also with the Air. This impurity <note>original: "unarth," referring to a base or degenerate nature.</note> is however so mixed with the others / that everything is immediately corrupted / and nothing can endure for long / since it causes death / and weakens all / yes even the Noble Natures: Understand this of all things / as well *Vegetable* <gloss>Plant-based substances.</gloss> / and *Animal* <gloss>Substances derived from living creatures.</gloss> / as *Mineral*. Therefore it is necessary to separate the Elemental nature from the corruptible / so that a thing may be brought to its healing power.

Within these three stones <note>The "three stones" likely refers to the Three Primes—Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—which, when unified, are sometimes referred to as the "stone" or essence of the three kingdoms of nature.</note> is everything that God has created; therefore, all things can be brought into a **crystalline clarity** through the **Art** <note>The "Art" is a standard term for the practice of Alchemy.</note> prescribed by the Philosophers. In alchemy, this color signifies the final stage of perfection and the completion of the Great Work.</note>. Hereafter, there shall be no more corruption, but rather an eternal **incorruptibility**. Do you think that everything the Lord GOD has created here below will perish in the last days? By no means; not the very least thing that GOD has created shall perish; just as little as the imperishable Heaven. But God, according to His good pleasure, will transform everything and make it crystalline, and the four elements shall become perfect, simple, and fixed, and everything shall be a **Fifth Essence** <term>Quinta essentia: The "quintessence," the purest state of matter where all elemental conflicts are resolved.</term>. Just as such can also be proven in this world through Art. For everything that GOD has created can [be brought] to **crystalline purity**...

# After the Reminders and Observations in the Previously Heard Teaching.

The natural, earthly-minded human is by nature so afflicted with passions <term>affects<gloss>from the Latin 'affectus,' referring to intense emotions or prejudices that cloud judgment</gloss></term> that he feels them in every occurrence, and can almost never be found with an impartial mind. Every person should constantly remind themselves of this; otherwise, it will lead to a great fall. ### Johann Isaac Hollandus

Therefore, I find in my own knowledge—which is likewise still in its first beginnings and eager to learn further—that everything which originates from Divine Creation is nothing but the strength and power of God the Most High, both the visible and the invisible. There is nothing created to be found or devised that is not either a substantial, tangible, hard and dry, or soft and fluid power—or else an invisible, intangible, spiritual power. 

All such powers, however innumerably manifold they may be, have their root and origin in the <term>Great Mystery<gloss>original: "Mysterio Magno"; a term used in alchemy and Paracelsian philosophy to describe the primordial source or "great womb" of all created things.</gloss></term>, which is the potential for all such powers. # The Vegetable Work

All plants—whether they be hot, cold, sweet, sour, poisonous, healing, or however they are natured—have their life and growth through the warmth of the single sun. And when autumn and winter take the warmth of the sun from them, they must all perish, even though their properties are entirely opposed to one another, yet they proceed from a single life. It is the same with all the forces that exist in the <term>nature of things<gloss>original: "rerum Natura"; a classical Latin term referring to the fundamental laws and substances of the physical world.</gloss></term>; they are all only one in their mother and <term>origin<gloss>original: "urstand"; the primal, unmanifested state of a substance.</gloss></term>. They are introduced into such <term>contrary<gloss>original: "wiederwertige"; meaning opposing or conflicting qualities like heat and cold.</gloss></term> properties for the reason that one should make the other all the more manifest. For how could one judge a sweet taste if the sour or sharp-salty were not known? How should one rightly recognize the lovely without the harsh and bitter, and so forth? 

Since such <term>opposites<gloss>original: "contraria"; the dialectical pairs that define the material world.</gloss></term> come from one source, and that source is only one and not two or more, there can be no discord in the "one." For it is only one and no more, and it has no counterpart. From this, it is to be understood that such opposition only arises or comes into being in the <term>revelation<gloss>original: "offenbahrung"; the process by which the hidden, unified essence of nature becomes visible and differentiated in the physical world.</gloss></term>. Furthermore, when it returns again to its beginning or its furthest end, it is no longer such an opposition. In this world, however, such opposition is found in all things, because God—to reveal His infinite wisdom—has ordained in Nature that she shall not rest for a single moment in bringing forth all kinds of colors, power, <term>virtue<gloss>original: "Tugend"; in an alchemical context, this refers to the inherent medicinal or spiritual potency of a substance.</gloss></term>, and wonders. This diversity requires that she make one thing hard, and the...

<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<summary>This page explains the alchemical principle of "Unity in Diversity," arguing that all plant life and natural forces stem from a single source (symbolized by the Sun) and that opposites like sweet and bitter exist only to make qualities perceptible to human senses.</summary>

...of the perfect human soul says, that it should be almighty in suffering, just as God is Almighty in acting. Because such a <term>Fifth Essence <note>original: "Quinta Essentia," the purest "fifth element" sought by alchemists to cure all ills and transmute metals.</note></term> is an indestructible, substantial life in which all contraries are united and brought into one simple essence: so it makes everything tempered and held in equal balance when it enters the human body. This is the opinion of Hollandus <note>Isaac Hollandus, a famous—though perhaps legendary—alchemist often cited in the 15th-17th centuries.</note>, which he discusses in detail here and there. To this, someone might object: Why then did all those who possessed the <term>true Fifth Essence <note>original: "veram Quintam Essentiam"</note></term>, and even Hollandus himself, die? Why did they not remain always in a balanced state <note>original: "temperatur"</note>, so that no death could have come upon them?

To this, the answer is that God has set a limit for man to live; the <term>Fifth Essence</term> cannot act against the Almighty Will of God. Just as at the time of the <term>Great Flood <note>original: "Sündflut"</note></term>, by God's command, the element of water predominated over the others; and afterwards, despite the fact that it had risen 15 cubits <note>original: "Elen," a historical unit of length, usually the distance from the elbow to the fingertips.</note> above the highest mountains around the earth, it nevertheless had to allow itself to be dried up again. To speak of the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: "Quinta Essentia," referring to the purest, most concentrated medicinal core of a substance</note>, the previously cited author likewise says in the very same little treatise: A dose taken of this glorious and highly-famed **Medicine** undoubtedly takes away from a person all incidental illness, renews the whole body, and keeps it <unclear>secure</unclear> from all heavy <unclear>shocks</unclear> <note>original: "Anstössen," which could refer to strokes, seizures, or sudden attacks of disease</note>; <unclear>for this Spiritual Medicine penetrates through marrow and bone, dispels the darkness of sick-</unclear>ness, and through its innate cleansing power leads out the cause of all ill-health in the most pleasant way. And although the **Counter-Chemists** <note>original: "Wider-Chemisten," those who opposed the new chemical and alchemical medicines of the era, often favoring traditional Galenic herbalism</note> hereupon commonly object that a saddle does not fit all horses, nor can one shoe be right for all feet—with which usual argument they intend to bring the unbelievable power of the <term>Universal Medicine <note>A "Panacea" or cure-all sought by alchemists that could treat any ailment by balancing the body's fundamental nature.</note></term> into ruin and doubt—yet the understanding and right-minded will easily judge what a great difference there is between a saddle and a **Spiritual Medicine** <note>In this context, "spiritual" refers to a medicine that is highly refined, volatile, and subtle, acting on the "vital spirits" of the body.</note>. Also, they must likewise grant me that a saddle fits many thousands of horses, and one shoe can be put on many thousands of feet. If now even such a thing is stood by and conceded regarding this medicine, then I have enough, for I do not say that all people [seek] help from this medicine...

# Vegetable-Work.

...[Hollandus] and others, who without any verbal revelation, but only through diligent reading of other Philosophers' books, arrive at the most secret jewel of the **Philosophers' Stone**. It takes quite a long time before some understanding comes from reading, but one must not let oneself be discouraged and should continue on until a proper grasp follows, even if it should only happen after several years. As for which books are good and useful for this purpose, I have partly specified them for you in my *Lamp of the Salt of the Philosophers* <note>original Latin: "Lucerna Salis Philosophorum," a work by the same author</note>, and also reminded you there to beware of **sophistical books**, among which are those of that same widely-renowned chemical writer **G.** <note>Likely referring to Johann Rudolf Glauber (1604–1670), a famous chemist whom the author accuses of "sophistry"—alchemical error—despite his fame.</note>. These words of mine, as I hear, were immediately misunderstood by many, as if I were seeking to belittle him in the minds of his admirers, which is by no means my *intent* <note>intention</note>; rather, I wish to put forward what I mean without regard for any specific person.

Since all *Philosophers* declare that to be **sophistry** which seeks something where it is not, or initiates some *process* <note>A chemical or alchemical procedure</note> against the orderly course of **Nature**—which He [Christ], through whom all things are made, Himself presents with this most philosophical teaching: *Can one gather grapes from thorns, or figs from thistles?* Thus, with my previous inquiry, I wanted to bring to the minds of the lovers of the blessed **Philosophical Work** that all those *published* *processes* by the mentioned *author* [G.], which were partly common before and partly newly *invented* by him, are to be considered **sophistry** if one wished to use them for this work. The reason: the ancient *Philosophers* knew nothing of such things, nor did they *distill* in such a manner,

--- Page 158 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on how the "Philosophers' Stone" is achieved through simple natural processes rather than complex laboratory manipulation. It explains the "sophistical" (deliberately misleading) writing style used by ancient masters to hide their secrets from the unworthy.</meta>

<page-num>140</page-num>

# Johannis Isaci Hollandi

...calcined, sublimated, imbibed, or the like, but rather simply—if they wished to propagate a thing—brought the determined seed of that thing, each according to its kind and gender, to its own matrix <note>The "matrix" is the environment or "womb" where a substance grows, such as the earth for a seed or a specific flask for a chemical.</note>, and proceeded with it according to nature, as the propagation of a human, grain, or plants demonstrates. And because the philosophical **Work of Regeneration** <note>original Latin: *Opus Regenerationis*; the alchemical process of purifying and perfecting matter.</note> is likewise founded upon the simple course of nature, being quite easy, simple, and plain in itself, yet next to the salvation of the soul is the highest gift of God in this world; therefore, the philosophers described it with nothing but flowery speech, so that those with evil intentions would be led astray by it, but the right-minded, with divine help prayed for, might pick out the simple truth. This is what causes all of today’s chemical **difficulties** <note>original Latin: *difficultæten*</note>; for their words are, according to the letter, entirely **sophistical** <note>In alchemy, "sophistry" refers to "blinds"—deliberately false instructions or confusing terminology meant to hide the truth from the uninitiated.</note> and so arranged that those intending to misuse them must necessarily be seduced thereby. However, according to the perceived meaning, they are truly **sophic** <note>Wise or containing true wisdom; from the Greek *sophia*.</note> to the **sons of doctrine** <note>original Latin: *filiis Doctrinæ*; a traditional term for initiated students of alchemy.</note>, and are nothing but true guidance to wisdom and its treasures.

**Geber** <note>An 8th-century alchemist (Jabir ibn Hayyan), notorious for his complex and often impenetrable style, which gave rise to the word "gibberish."</note> also masterfully applied himself to such a style, so that he himself says he wrote in such a way that his meaning would not remain hidden from the understanding, but would be difficult enough for the mediocre to grasp, and conversely should exclude the ignorant from his meaning quite miserably. Therefore, **Count Bernhard** <note>Bernhard of Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous alchemist who spent decades following "sophistical" (false) recipes before finding the truth.</note> also complains greatly about **Geber**, as well as **Archelaus**, **Rhazes**, **Rupescissa**, and others, that they intentionally mixed in such sophistical processes, upon which Bernhard himself stumbled greatly at the beginning. It was not that he intended to dishonor those same famous philosophers with such a word as "sophistry," for it is in itself not a defamatory word, as can be found in what the godly author of the **Way of Truth** <note>original Latin: *Viæ Veritatis*</note> (printed in the **Water-stone of the Wise** <note>original German: *Wasserstein der Weisen*, a famous alchemical compendium.</note>, page 210) writes of **Dr. Adam von Bodenstein** <note>A prominent 16th-century Paracelsian physician and editor of alchemical texts.</note>: that he was a master of sophistry and wrote many books on alchemy full of sophistical governance, which had the appearance of containing mighty understanding within them, and yet were unfounded things. He did not wish, however, with such words to slan- <note>The sentence continues on the next page; the catchword "stert" completes the word *gelästert*, meaning "slandered" or "belittled."</note>

<summary>This page explains that true alchemy follows the simple laws of nature rather than complex chemistry, but is hidden behind "sophistical" or coded language by masters like Geber to protect the secret from the wicked.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, nature, Geber, Bernhard of Trevisan, Adam von Bodenstein, sophistry, seed, matrix, Opus Regenerationis</keywords>

--- Page 159 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's warning against taking alchemical texts too literally. It builds on the previous page's discussion of "sophistry"—the practice of using deliberate "blinds" or misleading language to protect sacred knowledge from the unworthy.</meta>

<page-num>14</page-num>

-># Vegetable Work<-

...[analy]zed [the matter], but I only say this: that he was a **physician** <term>original Latin: *Medicus*; a doctor of medicine</term> and a good **sophist** <term>original Latin: *Sophist*; here meaning one who uses clever "blinds" or deceptive language to hide secrets</term>, but he did not truly understand the natural art of **alchemy** <term>original: *Alchimey*</term> or the **secrets of nature** <term>original Latin: *Secreta Naturæ*</term>, according to the evidence of his own writings. I therefore wish to warn the simple-minded—who build their hopes on such things—for their own good to beware of them; for the deception is great, but the truth is simple <note>original: *schlecht*; in this context, it means plain, simple, or straightforward rather than "bad."</note>.

I intended to indicate with my earlier reminder that every seeker should be cautious, and not easily work according to the literal letter of any **process** <term>original: *Processes*; a set of alchemical instructions or a recipe</term>. Instead, one should first labor with the brain and the senses to see if it can certainly succeed, and whether he has truly grasped the author's meaning before he loses hand-labor, expense, and time on it in vain. I do not wish to belittle that author or anyone else by this: for just as the literal content of his processes will not be found infallible by every reader following the instructions, so little should everything be discarded because of one or several errors. Rather, they should be considered collectively as ingenious speculations that can sharpen many toward greater discovery and knowledge. And I can say nothing else but that much good is contained in the said author's writings, especially in his **P. O.** <note>Possibly referring to the *Particularia* or a specific section of the work; in this context, it implies a valuable subset of recipes.</note>, a single part of which sufficiently pays back the value of what it cost.

However, the highly precious **Secret of the Philosophers** <term>original Latin: *Secretum Philoso-phorum*</term> is not to be sought in such processes, and even less should it be clearly described to everyone. Unless, of course, someone deliberately wants to bring the cruel curses of the philosophers upon his own neck; as **Rhazes** <term>original: *Rasis*; a 9th-century Persian polymath and alchemist</term> says: that he who would reveal this ultimate good to the unworthy shall be punished as a violator and tearer of the Divine Seal. **Raymond Lully** <term>original: *Raimund Lullius*; a major 13th-century philosopher and alchemist</term> says: He shall be damned at the Last Judgment. The author of the Great **Rosary** <term>original Latin: *Rosarii*; referring to the *Rosarium Philosophorum*, a famous 16th-century alchemical treatise</term> says: He shall be cursed and die of a stroke. **Basil Valentine** <term>original: *Basilius Valentinus*; a legendary, likely pseudonymous alchemical author</term> announces: Such a person is irreconcilable with God and handed over to the devil on every path and walkway. **Hollandus** <term>original: *Hollandus*; Isaac Hollandus, to whom this work is attributed</term> says here and there: He shall be tormented temporally and eternally. I, a **disciple** <term>original Latin: *Discipulus*</term> of these [who] in God...

<meta>catchword: rest</meta>

<summary>The author warns seekers not to follow alchemical recipes literally but to seek the underlying truth through reason. He emphasizes that the "Secret of the Philosophers" is deliberately hidden, and revealing it to the unworthy invites divine and earthly curses from the great masters.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, sophistry, Rhazes, Raymond Lully, Basil Valentine, Isaac Hollandus, Secretum Philosophorum, caution, deception</keywords>

--- Page 160 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's discourse on the necessity of a proper spiritual disposition when approaching alchemical secrets. It transitions from a discussion of faith and ethical conduct to a foundational definition of Isaac Hollandus's three-fold division of natural works.</meta>

# John Isaac Hollandus

men resting [in God] say Amen to this, and know that it will
not fail: For if GOD deemed the little faith / which
is only as large as a mustard seed / worthy / to move mountains / 
what then will He do upon the strong **Magical Faith** <term>original: *Magischen Glauben*; in alchemy, this refers to a focused, spiritualized will capable of influencing matter</term> 
of these men / as He sufficiently proved through Joshua with the 
halting of the sun.

Whether this, my dispassionate opinion, will be viewed by 
one or another in a bad or good light / can
give or take little or nothing from me / although I 
nevertheless prefer and wish / that no one takes 
any cause for resentment from this / my direct explanation / 
which would only be harmful to his own **inner man** <term>original: *innern Menschen*; a theological term for the soul or spiritual self</term>: 
Instead, each should as a Christian / fundamentally abolish 
all resentment, anger, and enmity / even against those who 
have offended him most highly / and reconcile himself with 
his counterpart / as if no ill-will had ever occurred 
between them / so that not even the smallest spark of 
displeasure or vexation remains in the memory on account 
of the offense; as long as someone does not do this / so long 
he has no true grace with GOD / but prays the **Lord’s Prayer** 
to his own damnation / since the heavenly Father / 
according to Christ’s words / will do unto us exactly / 
as we have done to our debtor; and this all the 
more / because He Himself preceded us in this / and through 
the sacrifice of His only Son / reconciled Himself with us / 
though He did not offend us / but we Him most highly / 
enough of this for now.

Now it remains that I also mention a little / in case 
someone does not yet know / what *Isaac Hollandus* means by 
writing three kinds of **Works** <term>original Latin: *Opera*</term> / such 
as **Animal, Vegetable** and **Mineral**: This, however, 
has this meaning / that all **sublunary** <term>original Latin: *sub-Lunarische*; meaning everything on Earth, beneath the celestial sphere of the Moon</term> 
things coming forth from the elements / are contained in three 
distinct kingdoms: Under the **Animal** <term>original Latin: *Animalia*</term> are 
all animals, worms, birds, fish, and everything that uses breath / 
along with all **substance** / that comes from and out of them / 
possessing life / movement / stirring / and sensation. To the 
**Vegetable** <term>original Latin: *Vegetabilia*</term> belongs everything that 
grows and turns green from the earth / in foliage /

<meta>catchword: Grass</meta>

<summary>The author argues that alchemical success requires a "Magical Faith" and a heart free from resentment, emphasizing Christian reconciliation. He then begins defining the Three Kingdoms of nature—Animal, Vegetable, and Mineral—which categorize all earthly matter.</summary>
<keywords>Magical Faith, Joshua, inner man, reconciliation, Lord's Prayer, Three Kingdoms, Animalia, Vegetabilia, sublunary elements</keywords>

--- Page 161 ---
<meta>This page continues the transition from the author's moral and spiritual exhortations to the practical foundations of alchemical study. Following the previous page's emphasis on Christian grace and the "inner man," the author now categorizes the natural world into three kingdoms—Animal, Vegetable, and Mineral—identifying the "noble subjects" within each that are used to create the three Alchemical Stones.</meta>

## Vegetable Work.
Aii

Grass, wood, stalks, straws, and what is found in them regarding roots, fruit, and other things. And such greening vegetables <note>original: *Vegetabilien*; here referring generally to the plant kingdom</note> also emerge in the water, such as duckweed <note>original: *Meerlinsen*; literally "sea lentils," a common historical term for duckweed</note> etc. By the **Mineral Kingdom**, one understands all those subjects that are coagulated within the earth, such as the ores of all metals and minerals, as well as all kinds of stone, and whatever is brought or boiled out of the earth from **mineral juices** <term>original: *Berg-Säfften*; literally "mountain juices," referring to liquid mineral solutions, vitriols, or dissolved salts found in mines</term>, such as sulfur, alum, etc. The most noble subjects of these three kingdoms are **Man**, **Wine**, and **Gold**, which stand in great congruence and kinship with one another, as the Philosophers long ago discovered and also taught: to prepare the **Animal Stone** from Man, the **Vegetable Stone** from Wine, and the **Mineral Stone** from the **Golden Being** or *star of the Sun* <note>original: *astro Solis*; a Latin term referring to the celestial essence or quintessential power residing within gold</note>. These three stones are unanimously acknowledged to possess all the power of the entire of Nature within themselves—and especially the last one, which, due to its mighty **projection** <term>the act of casting the alchemical powder or "stone" upon base metal to transmute it into gold</term>, is a truly Divine Mystery. (As we intend, God willing, to treat this in detail in our future *Harmony* <note>original: *Harmonia*; likely a reference to a specific treatise or a philosophical synthesis planned by the author</note>.) Hollandus treats all of this in his writings with full detail, and has gathered the *great wonders* <note>original: *magnalia*; Latin for "great things" or miraculous operations</note> and secret operations known to him from each kingdom into a special Work; however, he occasionally moves from one to another and explains one through the other. I highly wish this to be well understood by the reader, and—

->closing herewith, I heartily commend him<-
->under the wings of grace of the<-
->**Heavenly Brood-Hen**, <note>A mystical/pietistic metaphor for Divine protection and the nurturing of spiritual knowledge, often associated with the Holy Spirit or Wisdom.</note> for all<-
->necessary knowledge.<-

<image-desc>A decorative printer's ornament consisting of a central small circle flanked by two symmetrical floral or leafy flourishes.</image-desc>

<summary>The author defines the three alchemical kingdoms—Vegetable, Mineral, and Animal—identifying Man, Wine, and Gold as their primary subjects for creating alchemical elixirs. The text concludes by commending the reader to Divine guidance and the writings of Isaac Hollandus.</summary>

<keywords>Three Kingdoms of Nature, Animal Stone, Vegetable Stone, Mineral Stone, Gold, Isaac Hollandus, Alchemical projection, Astro Solis, Magnalia, Heavenly Brood-Hen</keywords>

--- Page 162 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the author’s philosophical and alchemical discourse to a direct moral address. Having previously discussed the "Vegetable," "Animal," and "Mineral" works, the author now addresses those who might mock or suppress his writings, advocating for religious tolerance and universal love as the true foundation of a godly life.</meta>

(144)

## To the Scorners.

<image-desc>Decorative ornaments flank the page number at the top. A large ornamental woodcut initial 'O' begins the first paragraph.</image-desc>

**O**lthough it causes no loss to my health or condition if someone holds or makes what I have written contemptible; nevertheless, to everyone for whom this might not be to their liking (as it is impossible to please all temperaments <term>original: *complexionen*; referring to the four bodily humors or psychological dispositions of a person</term> at once with a single work), it is my friendly exhortation that they should not demonstrate their partisan mind against it, but at the very least show humanity <term>original: *humanitæt*; here meaning common courtesy or kindness</term> and not hinder others who view it more gladly than they do, so that it may reach their hands without contradiction. This, to my knowledge, does him no harm, unless it were that he enjoys some easily-diminished private profit and fears that it might be hindered herewith.

To the others, however, for whom this is not only pleasing but who also feel a testimony within their conscience <note>original: *Mitzeugnuß ihres Gewissens*</note> that it is the truth, I ask—by our dear God and Lord, who provides for all people in the entire world with equal love—that they strive and seek with all the powers of their understanding and ability to find the means and ways by which love among people, one for another, and also the love of people toward God might be further kindled. For at this current time, love is almost entirely extinguished and has transformed into an inhuman cruelty of one brother against another.

In my estimation, the priests and teachers of all religions and sects—none excluded—bear no small amount of guilt for this. They keep the hearts of their listeners always preoccupied <note>original: *præoccupirt*; meaning prejudiced or already filled with an idea</note> and filled with hatred and disgust toward those who are not of their confession <term>the specific religious denomination or creed one belongs to</term>. Were it not for this, there would sometimes be better and friendlier blood among men; just as nature teaches almost all nations this truth by allowing the produce, goods, and manufactures of their land to flow to other nations regardless of what religion they are. Likewise, they gladly accept and use the gifts of God that come to them from other lands. 

Indeed, many quarrelsome teachers themselves likely drink the wine and beverages of the very kingdom whose inhabitants they consider damned because of "incorrect" religion, as they see it. Nor do they despise a people’s minted coin for their own necessities, even though they recognize them as heretics <term>original: *Ketzer*; those whose religious beliefs differ from the established or "orthodox" church</term>. Yet, they are nonetheless sometimes so fierce and embittered toward those fellow human beings who seem heretical to them—whose land's produce, wine, money, and cloth they themselves use—that they would not be unwilling to see them completely rooted out. This, however, is entirely against God; and those who wish to be born of Him must also be full of divine mercy <note>original: *Barmherzigkeit*</note> toward both friends and enemies. May those who love the truth strive to help spread this spirit.

<summary>The author delivers a stern critique of religious intolerance, arguing that priests and teachers foster unnecessary hatred between denominations. He points out the hypocrisy of those who condemn "heretics" while still enjoying their trade goods, wine, and currency.</summary>
<keywords>religious tolerance, hypocrisy, brotherly love, sectarianism, humanity, commerce, divine mercy</keywords>
